([tech.hancom.com](https://tech.hancom.com/hwpxformat/))  \n\nSo: if you unzip a real `.hwpx` and look at the root `mimetype` file, **seeing `application/hwp+zip` is expected** in many implementations/references.\n\n---\n\n## 2) Why you might see `application/vnd.hancom.hwpx` in web frameworks / whitelists\n\nSome developer posts list `.hwpx` as:\n\n- `application/vnd.hancom.hwpx`\n- `application/haansofthwpx`\n\nExample:\n\n- A blog post about configuring MIME whitelists (CodeIgniter) lists:  \n  `hwpx : 'application/vnd.hancom.hwpx', 'application/haansofthwpx'` ([devshoveling.tistory.com](https://devshoveling.tistory.com/entry/%ED%95%9C%EA%B8%80-MIME-type-feat-Codeigniter?utm_source=openai))  \n\nThis helps explain why your query includes `application/vnd.hancom.hwpx`: **it’s commonly used in practice for upload filtering or extension→MIME mappings**, even if it doesn’t match the internal `mimetype` file content inside the package.\n\n---\n\n## 3) What to use for HTTP `Content-Type` for `.hwpx` (per Hancom developer forum)\n\nA separate but closely related question appears in Hancom’s developer forum: whether HTTP `Content-Type: application/hwp+zip` is okay.\n\n- Hancom forum response: **HWPX does not have an “officially registered” MIME type**, and:\n  - If you have a custom client behavior for HWPX, you can use `application/hwp+zip`\n  - Otherwise, `application/octet-stream` is also fine (because HWPX is “zip binary”). ([forum.developer.hancom.com](https://forum.developer.hancom.com/t/hwpx-http-content-type-application-hwp-zip/1752))  \n\nSo, in HTTP responses/downloads, you may see either:\n- `application/hwp+zip` (custom/conventional), or\n- `application/octet-stream` (generic safe default), or\n- a nonstandard vendor type like `application/vnd.hancom.hwpx` depending on server/framework defaults.\n\n---\n\n## 4) Background: what HWPX is (structure / standard)\n\nSearch results also consistently describe:\n\n- HWPX is **XML-based** and **ZIP-packaged** (so you can rename `.hwpx` to `.zip` and inspect contents). ([tech.hancom.com](https://tech.hancom.com/hwpxformat/))  \n- HWPX is based on **OWPML** and registered as **KS X 6101** (Korean industrial standard). ([hancom.com](https://www.hancom.com/support/faqCenter/faq/detail/2784?utm_source=openai))  \n\n---\n\n## Practical interpretation (why your exact phrase is “conflicting”)\n\nIf your goal is:\n\n### ([tech.hancom.com](https://tech.hancom.com/hwpxformat/))  \n\nSo: if you unzip a real `.hwpx` and look at the root `mimetype` file, **seeing `application/hwp+zip` is expected** in many implementations/references.\n\n---\n\n## 2) Why you might see `application/vnd.hancom.hwpx` in web frameworks / whitelists\n\nSome developer posts list `.hwpx` as:\n\n- `application/vnd.hancom.hwpx`\n- `application/haansofthwpx`\n\nExample:\n\n- A blog post about configuring MIME whitelists (CodeIgniter) lists:  \n  `hwpx : 'application/vnd.hancom.hwpx', 'application/haansofthwpx'` ([devshoveling.tistory.com](https://devshoveling.tistory.com/entry/%ED%95%9C%EA%B8%80-MIME-type-feat-Codeigniter?utm_source=openai))  \n\nThis helps explain why your query includes `application/vnd.hancom.hwpx`: **it’s commonly used in practice for upload filtering or extension→MIME mappings**, even if it doesn’t match the internal `mimetype` file content inside the package.\n\n---\n\n## 3) What to use for HTTP `Content-Type` for `.hwpx` (per Hancom developer forum)\n\nA separate but closely related question appears in Hancom’s developer forum: whether HTTP `Content-Type: application/hwp+zip` is okay.\n\n- Hancom forum response: **HWPX does not have an “officially registered” MIME type**, and:\n  - If you have a custom client behavior for HWPX, you can use `application/hwp+zip`\n  - Otherwise, `application/octet-stream` is also fine (because HWPX is “zip binary”). ([forum.developer.hancom.com](https://forum.developer.hancom.com/t/hwpx-http-content-type-application-hwp-zip/1752))  \n\nSo, in HTTP responses/downloads, you may see either:\n- `application/hwp+zip` (custom/conventional), or\n- `application/octet-stream` (generic safe default), or\n- a nonstandard vendor type like `application/vnd.hancom.hwpx` depending on server/framework defaults.\n\n---\n\n## 4) Background: what HWPX is (structure / standard)\n\nSearch results also consistently describe:\n\n- HWPX is **XML-based** and **ZIP-packaged** (so you can rename `.hwpx` to `.zip` and inspect contents). ([tech.hancom.com](https://tech.hancom.com/hwpxformat/))  \n- HWPX is based on **OWPML** and registered as **KS X 6101** (Korean industrial standard). ([hancom.com](https://www.hancom.com/support/faqCenter/faq/detail/2784?utm_source=openai))  \n\n---\n\n## Practical interpretation (why your exact phrase is “conflicting”)\n\nIf your goal is:\n\n### A) Validate an uploaded `.hwpx` by inspecting file contents\nThe strongest “content-based” indicator cited in Hancom’s forum is:\n- unzip container → check the root `mimetype` file → expect **`application/hwp+zip`** ([forum.developer.hancom.com](https://forum.developer.hancom.com/t/hwp-hwpx-mime-type-whitelist/1641))\n\nIn that scenario, expecting the internal `mimetype` file to contain `application/vnd.hancom.hwpx` may cause false negatives.\n\n### B) Configure a server/framework MIME mapping for `.hwpx`\nYou may encounter (or choose) MIME labels like:\n- `application/vnd.hancom.hwpx` (common in framework whitelists) ([devshoveling.tistory.com](https://devshoveling.tistory.com/entry/%ED%95%9C%EA%B8%80-MIME-type-feat-Codeigniter?utm_source=openai))  \n- `application/hwp+zip` (often referenced for HWPX; also used internally) ([forum.developer.hancom.com](https://forum.developer.hancom.com/t/hwp-hwpx-mime-type-whitelist/1641))  \n- `application/octet-stream` (safe fallback recommended by Hancom forum reply) ([forum.developer.hancom.com](https://forum.developer.hancom.com/t/hwpx-http-content-type-application-hwp-zip/1752))  \n\n---\n\nIf you tell me which of these you’re trying to do—**(1) detect/validate file type from bytes**, **(2) set HTTP `Content-Type`**, or **(3) build an upload whitelist**—I can suggest a concrete rule set (including what to check first and what to accept as alternates)."],"durationSeconds":42.277755019999994,"searchCount":0}}

avoid prefix rewriting.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    # Skip XML declaration and comments/doctype; find first real start tag.\n    m = re.search(r\"<([^!?][^\\s>/]+)([^>]*)>\", xml_text)\n    if not m:\n Determine hp namespace URI; fallback to known.\n    hp_uri = ns.get(\"hp\", \"http://www.hancom.co.kr/hwpml/2010/HWPML\")\n    run_tag = f\"{{{hp_uri}}}run\"\n    t_tag = f\"{{{hp_uri}}}t\"\n\n    runs = [c for c in list(p_el) if c.tag == run_tag]\n    if not runs:\n        run = ET.Element(run_tag)\n        p_el.insert(0, run)\n        runs = [run]\n\n    # Keep first run, drop the rest (but \"t\":\n            # keep non-t formatting nodes; but in this fixture none exist.\n            continue\n\n    return first_t\n\n\ndef replace_text_everywhere(\n    root: ET.Element,\n    replacements: Sequence[Tuple[str, str]],\n    skip_paragraphs_with_exact_text: Set[str],\n) -> Tuple[int, Set[ET.Element]]:\n    \"\"\"Replace occurrences in .text and .tail across the tree.\n\n    Returns: (replacement_count, modified_paragraph_elements)\n    \"\"\"\n\n    parent_map = build_parent_map(root)\n    modified_paras: Set[ET.Element] = set()\n    count = 0\n\n    # For skipping, we     modified_paras.add(cur)\n                return\n            cur = parent_map.get(cur)\n\n    for el in root.iter():\n        if id(el) in skip_para_ids:\n            # Don't touch anything inside the appendix paragraph.\n            continue\n\n        if el.text:\n            new_text = el.text\n            for old, new in replacements:\n                if old and old in new_text:\n                    new_text = new_text.replace(old, new)\n            if new_text != el.text:\n                el.text = new_text\n                count += 1\n                note_modified(el)\n\n        if
